Bash: Counterchange against actual water background
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open('grasslod/painter.py').read()
s=s.replace("    bgv[R.water] = 4.6      # water and sky read as light behind grass","    bgv = water_background(R, V, bgv)   # water: light sky tint, or the dark mirrored bank")
s+='''

def water_background(R, V, bgv, sky_value=4.6):
    """What a blade crossing the water is seen against: the mirrored bank above the far
    waterline (its design value, lights dropped), else the light sky tint."""
    H, W = V.shape
    wat = R.water
    out = bgv.copy()
    for c in range(W):
        col = wat[:, c]
        if not col.any():
            continue
        r = 0
        while r < H:
            if not col[r]:
                r += 1
                continue
            r0 = r
            while r < H and col[r]:
                r += 1
            for rr in range(r0, r):
                src = r0 - 1 - (rr - r0)
                if src >= 0 and R.mat[src, c] in (1, 3):
                    out[rr, c] = min(V[src, c], 2.0)
                else:
                    out[rr, c] = sky_value
    return out
'''
open('grasslod/painter.py','w').write(s)
EOF

Write: elements/grass-lod/src/vocab.py
"""vocab.py: the mark vocabulary as a function of projected blade length ell.

A flat lit patch and a flat shadowed patch, painted at a ladder of ell, each at 4x, plus a
'handoff strip': one tuft population drawn at 24 ell steps so you can follow single tufts
shrinking from blades to ticks to dots to tone.
"""
import os, sys
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from grasslod.world import World
from grasslod.render import Camera
from grasslod.marks import tuft_population, paint_tufts, glyph_area, MarkParams
from grasslod.world import SPECIES
from grasslod.calibrate import correction, _Flat
from grasslod.noise import stable_threshold
from grasslod import palette as P


def flat_patch(ell, v, litfam, hour="golden", W=64, H=48, theta=30.0, mp=None, world=None):
    w = world or World(seed=1)
    import copy
    fw = copy.copy(w)
    fw.height = lambda x, y, min_wl=0.0: np.zeros(np.shape(x))
    fw.water_level = -10
    mp = mp or MarkParams()
    sp = SPECIES[w.species]
    hmean = sp[0] * (1 - 0.75 * w.grazing)
    c, s = np.cos(np.radians(theta)), np.sin(np.radians(theta))
    ppm = ell / (hmean * np.sqrt(c * c + (mp["lean_top"] * s ** 3) ** 2))
    cam = Camera(ppm=ppm, theta=theta, W=W, H=H)
    R = _Flat(); R.cam = cam; R.min_wl = 1 / ppm; R.sun_screen_x = -1
    xs = (np.arange(W) + 0.5 - W / 2) / ppm
    ys = ((H / 2 - (np.arange(H) + 0.5)) / ppm) / s
    R.X, R.Y = np.meshgrid(xs, ys)
    R.depth = R.Y.copy(); R.mat = np.ones((H, W), np.int8)
    L = np.full((H, W), litfam)
    V0 = np.full((H, W), v)
    Vd = V0 - correction(fw, mp, ell, V0, L, mp["ell_ref"])
    rho = 1.0 / (sp[1] ** 2 * ppm ** 2 * s)
    cov = mp["coverage"] * np.clip(ell / 0.6, 0.35, 1.0) + mp["overlap"] * np.clip((ell - 1.5) / 4.0, 0, 1)
    keep = min(1.0, cov / (rho * glyph_area(ell)))
    m = 3 * sp[0] + 3 / ppm
    pop = tuft_population(fw, xs.min() - m, xs.max() + m, ys.min() - m, ys.max() + 3 * m, min(1, keep * 1.8), seed=7)
    canvas = np.full((H, W), np.nan); cm = np.zeros((H, W), np.int8); pr = np.full((H, W), -1, np.int64)
    if pop is not None:
        pop["keep"] = keep
        paint_tufts(canvas, cm, pr, R, Vd, L, fw, pop, mp, fam_top=(2.9, 6.0), fam_bot=(0, 0))
    lo = np.floor(Vd); f = Vd - lo
    bw = mp["band_w"]
    g = np.clip((f - bw / 2) / max(1 - bw, 1e-3), 0, 1)
    qb = lo + (g > stable_threshold(R.X, R.Y * s, ppm, px=1.0))
    q = np.where(np.isnan(canvas), qb, np.floor(np.nan_to_num(canvas) + 0.5))
    q = np.clip(q, 0, 6).astype(int)
    pal, _ = P.build(hour)
    return P.to_rgb(P.GREEN0 + q, pal)


def main(hour="golden"):
    ells = [14, 9, 6, 4, 2.8, 2.0, 1.4, 1.0, 0.7, 0.45, 0.25, 0.12]
    k = 4
    W, H = 64, 48
    rows = [("lit, V=4.8", 4.8, True), ("lit, V=4.1", 4.1, True), ("shadow, V=1.0", 1.0, False)]
    im = Image.new("RGB", (100 + len(ells) * (W * k + 4), 24 + len(rows) * (H * k + 4)), (24, 24, 28))
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(im)
    for j, e in enumerate(ells):
        d.text((100 + j * (W * k + 4) + 4, 6), f"ell {e:g} px", fill=(220, 220, 220))
    for i, (lab, v, lf) in enumerate(rows):
        d.text((6, 24 + i * (H * k + 4) + 6), lab, fill=(220, 220, 220))
        for j, e in enumerate(ells):
            t = flat_patch(e, v, lf, hour, W, H)
            im.paste(Image.fromarray(t).resize((W * k, H * k), Image.NEAREST), (100 + j * (W * k + 4), 24 + i * (H * k + 4)))
    out = os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), "..", "img", f"vocab_{hour}.png")
    im.save(out)
    print("wrote", out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main(*(sys.argv[1:] or ["golden"]))

    """Designed fraction of pixels covered by marks, as a smooth function of stroke size:
    near strands ~0.58 (Ferrari's lit bank leaves ~half its base showing), ticks ~0.4,
    thinning to a light peppering far off, where tone takes over."""
    ell = np.asarray(ell, float)
    return (mp["cov_far"] + (mp["cov_mid"] - mp["cov_far"]) * np.clip(ell / 1.5, 0, 1)
            + (mp["cov_near"] - mp["cov_mid"]) * np.clip((ell - 1.5) / 4.5, 0, 1))


def keep_for(world, mp, ell, ppm, s, ratio=None):
    """Fraction of tufts to keep so that marks cover target_coverage(ell) of the pixels.
    glyph_area is a guess; `ratio` (measured coverage / nominal, from calibrate) fixes it."""
    sp = SPECIES[world.species]
    rho = 1.0 / (sp[1] ** 2 * ppm ** 2 * max(s, 0.05))
    if ratio is None:
        from .calibrate import coverage_ratio
        ratio = coverage_ratio(world, mp, ell)
    return float(min(1.0, target_coverage(mp, ell) / (rho * glyph_area(ell) * max(ratio, 0.05))))

_COV = {}


def coverage_ratio(world, mp, ell):
    """Measured / nominal mark coverage at stroke length ell (glyph_area is only a guess:
    blades of one tuft overlap, short blades round to nothing, ...)."""
    k = "cov" + _key(world, mp)
    if k not in _COV:
        fn = os.path.join(CACHE_DIR, f"cov_{_key(world, mp)}.npy")
        if os.path.exists(fn):
            _COV[k] = np.load(fn)
        else:
            from .marks import tuft_population, paint_tufts, keep_for, target_coverage
            r = np.ones(len(ELLS))
            for ie, e in enumerate(ELLS):
                fw, cam, R, box = _flat_setup(world, mp, e)
                keep = keep_for(world, mp, e, cam.ppm, cam.s, ratio=1.0)
                pop = tuft_population(fw, *box, min(1.0, keep * 1.8), seed=7)
                H, W = R.X.shape
                canvas = np.full((H, W), np.nan)
                if pop is not None:
                    pop["keep"] = keep
                    paint_tufts(canvas, np.zeros((H, W), np.int8), np.full((H, W), -1, np.int64), R,
                                np.full((H, W), 4.3), np.ones((H, W), bool), fw, pop, mp, fam_top=(2.9, 6.0), fam_bot=(0, 0))
                meas = (~np.isnan(canvas[10:-4, 8:-8])).mean()
                nominal = min(1.0, target_coverage(mp, e) / max(keep, 1e-9) * keep) if keep < 1 else meas
                r[ie] = np.clip(meas / max(target_coverage(mp, e), 1e-6), 0.05, 5.0) if keep < 1 else 1.0
            os.makedirs(CACHE_DIR, exist_ok=True)
            np.save(fn, r)
            _COV[k] = r
    le = np.log(np.clip(ell, ELLS[0], ELLS[-1]))
    return float(np.interp(le, np.log(ELLS), _COV[k]))

Bash: Reference board figure
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && cat > figs.py <<'EOF'
"""figs.py: the stage figures for LESSONS.md (reference board, 8x comparisons)."""
import os
import numpy as np
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
HERE = os.path.dirname(__file__)
ST = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "..", "..")
IMG = os.path.join(HERE, "..", "img")
LW = os.path.join(ST, "pedagogy", "master-copy", "ref", "lw")
NAT = os.path.join(ST, "pedagogy", "master-copy", "ref", "native.png")
V04 = os.path.join(ST, "grass", "refs", "ferrari_V04.png")


def crop(path, x, y, w, h, k):
    im = Image.open(path).convert("RGB").crop((x, y, x + w, y + h))
    return im.resize((w * k, h * k), Image.NEAREST)


def board(items, cols, out, title, bg=(24, 24, 28)):
    ims = [(lab, im) for lab, im in items]
    cw = max(im.width for _, im in ims); ch = max(im.height for _, im in ims)
    rows = (len(ims) + cols - 1) // cols
    B = Image.new("RGB", (cols * (cw + 8) + 8, 24 + rows * (ch + 22)), bg)
    d = ImageDraw.Draw(B); d.text((8, 6), title, fill=(230, 230, 230))
    for i, (lab, im) in enumerate(ims):
        x = 8 + (i % cols) * (cw + 8); y = 24 + (i // cols) * (ch + 22)
        d.text((x, y), lab, fill=(200, 200, 200)); B.paste(im, (x, y + 14))
    B.save(out)


def refs():
    items = [
        ("Ferrari V16: near shadow bank, olive strands on black (4x)", crop(f"{LW}/V16.png", 470, 360, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari tarn: near lit bank, dark strands on ochre (4x)", crop(NAT, 10, 320, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari tarn: crest, isolated dark holes -> ticks (4x)", crop(NAT, 10, 252, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari V16: lit bank crest, picket of lit ticks (4x)", crop(f"{LW}/V16.png", 0, 232, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari V16: mid bank, 3-green checker domes (4x)", crop(f"{LW}/V16.png", 520, 225, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari V16: islet, 2-colour checker, fringed crest (4x)", crop(f"{LW}/V16.png", 255, 255, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari V04: far slopes, flat planes, fine texture (4x)", crop(V04, 300, 250, 100, 70, 4)),
        ("Ferrari V04: near blades, curved, 2px (4x)", crop(V04, 350, 350, 100, 70, 4)),
    ]
    board(items, 4, os.path.join(IMG, "stage0_ferrari_distance_ladder.png"),
          "Stage 0: how Ferrari paints grass at each distance (crops from his native pixels)")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    refs()
EOF

Bash: Perspective bank comparison
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && cat >> figs.py <<'EOF'


def perspective_bank(hour="golden", v=4.8):
    """Emulate a bank receding in perspective: horizontal bands whose ell grows downward
    (0.5 px at the top -> 9 px at the bottom), next to Ferrari's tarn bank, crest to near."""
    import vocab
    W, H, band = 100, 150, 10
    ells = np.geomspace(0.5, 9.0, H // band)
    rows = []
    for i, e in enumerate(ells):
        t = vocab.flat_patch(float(e), v, True, hour, W=W, H=band + 24)
        rows.append(t[12:12 + band])
    mine = Image.fromarray(np.concatenate(rows, 0)).resize((W * 4, H * 4), Image.NEAREST)
    ref = crop(NAT, 10, 252, W, H, 4)
    board([("mine: bands of growing ell (0.5 -> 9 px), golden", mine), ("Ferrari tarn bank: crest -> near", ref)], 2,
          os.path.join(IMG, "stage2_perspective_bank.png"), "Stage 2: the handoff inside one receding bank, 4x")
EOF

    # the rate scales with tuft density on screen relative to the calibration (theta 30)

m=open('grasslod/marks.py').read()
m=m.replace('''    v = v + mp["crown_light"] * (cl - 0.5) * lt

    v = np.floor(v + U[12])''','''    # each tuft stands on the *same integer base step the base layer shows at its root*,
    # so its strokes are whole steps away from what surrounds them (a stroke equal to the
    # local base would be invisible) and a zone keeps Ferrari's ~4 colours
    if getattr(R, "qbase", None) is not None:

def quantize_base(Vd, X, Y, ppm, s, band_w):
    """Base layer: flat bands of whole steps, dithered only across the seams, with a
    world-anchored threshold (no shimmer on zoom)."""
    from .noise import stable_threshold
    lo = np.floor(Vd)
    f = Vd - lo
    g = np.clip((f - band_w / 2) / max(1 - band_w, 1e-3), 0, 1)
    return (lo + (g > stable_threshold(X, Y * s, ppm, px=1.0))).astype(int)
'''

# Grass at every distance: a study of the handoff

*Grass that zooms continuously from a person 30 px tall (30 px/m) to a whole region
(0.3 px/m) without popping, in Mark Ferrari's painted-pixel language.*

Everything here is made by `src/` (package `grasslod/`).  `src/render_all.sh` regenerates
every image.  Paths below are relative to this folder.

**Deliverables**

- `img/zoom_golden.gif`, `img/zoom_day.gif`: 96-frame continuous zooms, 30 to 0.3 px/m, 2x.
  Filmstrips `img/zoom_*_strip.png`, pop metrics `img/zoom_*_metrics.png`.
- `img/vocab_golden.png`, `img/vocab_day.png`: the mark vocabulary as a function of stroke size.
- `img/stage2_perspective_bank.png`: the handoff inside one receding bank, beside Ferrari's.
- `sheets/sheet_A..F`: hour, species, moisture x grazing, wind, camera angle, seeds, each
  across distance.
- The technique: `grasslod.paint(world, camera, hour, vparams, mparams)`.

---

## 0. What Ferrari does at each distance

![ladder](img/stage0_ferrari_distance_ladder.png)

I cropped every grass passage I could find in his native pixels (Mirror Pond V16, the tarn,
the rain scene V04) and ordered them by distance.  Read top-left to bottom-right, they are
one continuous vocabulary:

| distance | what the pixels are | colours |
|---|---|---|
| near, lit (tarn bank) | 1-px vertical strands, 4-10 px, of the *darker* steps on a lit base; ~half the base shows | 3-4 |
| near, shadow (V16) | 1-px olive strands on black, leaning, curving | 3 |
| crest of a receding bank (tarn) | **isolated black holes** on ochre at the top, turning into brown 1x2-1x4 ticks lower down, then strands | 3 |
| lit crest over a shadowed body (V16) | a picket of bright ticks along the crest only, thinning into the dark body | 4 |
| middle distance, shade (V16 mid bank) | a checker of 3 close greens; darker fold bands; form by density | 3 |
| far, lit (islet) | 2-colour checker, form turned by the density of the lit colour; fringed crest | 2-3 |
| far slopes (V04) | flat value planes, dither only at their seams, faint fine texture | 2-3 per plane |

Three things in this table decided the design:

1. **The tarn bank already *is* a handoff.**  Top to bottom it runs crest (far) to near, and
   the vocabulary changes continuously inside one mass: holes, ticks, strands.  Ferrari
   solved the within-picture handoff; a zoom has to do the same thing in time.
2. **The minority-stroke rule.**  In a lit mass the marks are the darks; in a shadowed mass
   they are the lights.  The base is the majority colour, flat.
3. **Far off, only the extremes survive.**  At the crest of the tarn bank the marks are the
   *deepest* dark (the holes between tufts), not the mid tone.  The mid-tone ticks appear
   only once blades are resolvable.  I had this backwards for a long time (see section 4).

Photos (`photos/*/_sheet.jpg`) agreed on the structure behind this: close up, blades cross
and arc; at 10 m grass is combed streaks with lighter tips and clumps; at 100 m it is
patches (straw on dry ground, green in hollows); from the air it is tone and mottling on
landform, with no blades at all.

---

## 1. The idea: one number per tuft, and every decision dithered

Grass is painted as **tufts**: objects in the world, each with its own sixteen stable random
numbers.  One continuous quantity per tuft decides how it is painted: its projected blade
length in pixels,

    ell = h * ppm * sqrt(cos(theta)^2 + (lean * sin(theta)^3)^2)

(h the local blade height, theta the camera elevation; the second term lets a top-down
camera still see tufts as small radiating marks).

The vocabulary is a function of ell:

- ell >= ~5: a tuft is a fan of 1-px blades (count ~ ell/1.6), stacked in adjacent steps,
  with a partner column (stem) and sometimes a dark gap column.
- ell 2-5: short strokes and ticks, one or two per tuft.
- ell ~1-2: dabs (a lit pip over a dark pip) and 1x2 ticks.
- ell < 1: single dots.  On lit ground a dot is a hole (the deepest dark); on shadowed
  ground a light speck.
- Far below 1: the dots thin out and the base layer (flat planes, dithered only at their
  seams) carries the picture.

**The handoff rule.**  Every *discrete* choice (how many blades, how many whole pixels long,
pair or no pair, gap or no gap, dark stroke or light, drawn or dropped) compares a smooth
function of ell against *one of that tuft's own random numbers*.  So as the camera zooms,
each tuft changes at its own moment and the vocabulary dissolves instead of switching.  It is
dithering, applied to the mark vocabulary itself.  (The first version had one hard threshold
left in it, the width term of the coverage estimate at ell = 3, and the metric found it as a
single tone spike at 12.5 px/m. Removing it removed the spike.)

**Population.**  Tufts live on a nested hierarchy of jittered grids: level k has one tuft per
cell of size s0 * 2^k with ranks in [4^-(k+1), 4^-k).  Keeping ranks below K keeps a fraction
K of all tufts, the kept set only grows as K grows, and at a distance only the coarse levels
are enumerated.  Zooming out, tufts drop out one by one, lowest rank last, and each survivor
stands for more grass.

**Coverage is designed, then measured.**  The fraction of pixels covered by marks is a smooth
design curve: ~0.55 near (Ferrari's lit bank leaves about half its base showing), 0.40 for
ticks, thinning to 0.12 far off.  How many tufts that takes can't be computed (blades of a
tuft overlap each other, marks overlap marks), so it is measured once on a flat patch:
coverage = 1 - exp(-a * keep), and the rate a(ell) is stored and inverted
(`calibrate.coverage_ratio`).

**Tone lock.**  Each vocabulary has a different mean offset from the design value (dark gaps
pull a lit mass down; clipped highlights can't push it up).  Measured once on flat patches
for a grid of ell and design values, and every frame paints with
V' = V - (offset(ell) - offset(ell_ref)).  A patch's squinted tone at 0.3 px/m is its
squinted tone at 30 px/m.  Without it the far views were a full step lighter than the near.

**World-anchored everything.**  Terrain and every ecological field are band-limited
functions of world position (octaves below two pixels fade out, never cut).  The base
layer's seam dither uses a threshold anchored to the ground (`noise.stable_threshold`: world
value noise whose lattice level cross-fades between powers of two as the zoom changes, the
surface-stable fractal dithering idea), and so do the water's wind dashes.  Nothing is
screen-random, so nothing shimmers.

**Measuring pops.**  `zoom.py` compares each frame, shrunk about its centre, with the next
frame's centre: tone = |mean luminance difference|, structure = mean |blur2 difference|.  The
structure floor of *perfect* nearest-neighbour zooming of one frame is 3.3-4.3 on this scale
(I measured it), so only spikes above that mean anything.

![metrics](img/zoom_golden_metrics.png)

---

## 2. The value design is the painting (and the far views are nothing else)

`render.value_design` produces a continuous step coordinate V per pixel.  Things I had to add
before anything read, in the order I found them:

- **Two families that never touch**: lit 3..5 (6 reserved for sun-struck tips on crests),
  shadow 0.2..2.8.  The family boundary is dithered by a world-anchored threshold over a soft
  light weight, so terminators and shadow edges are fringes, not cut-outs.
- **Exaggerated facing**: slope-to-sun differences relative to flat ground, x2 at the pixel
  scale and again at ~30 px (bank and hill scale).  Physical Lambert on an ortho 30 degree
  view is flat everywhere; a gentle hummock must read.
- **Masses made of masses**: convexity summed at 6, 16 and 48 px.  Crests lighter, folds
  darker, at every zoom.
- **Mottling**: tone patches at 8-60 m (colonies, wet flushes) - the texture of grassland
  seen from far.
- **Cast shadows fixed in world units** (geometric march 5 cm - 80 m), and **simplified like
  a painter's**: relief smaller than 8 px casts none.  Two bugs here were instructive.  When
  the march reach scaled with zoom, things fell into shadow as you zoomed out (a slow pop).
  When the ray started at the fine surface but was tested against band-limited samples,
  every fine hollow filled with false shadow and far views turned to leopard camouflage.
- **Flat planes at every zoom**: the base is quantized into whole steps with dither only in a
  seam band (band_w 0.6).  Full stochastic dither far off read as sandpaper; flat planes read
  as Ferrari's V04 slopes.

---

## 3. Near: blades, fringes, counterchange (stage 1)

![vocab](img/vocab_golden.png)

At 30 px/m a meadow blade is 8 px (0.3 m at 30 degrees): the tarn-bank regime, not V04's
giant curving blades.  Matching that bank at 8x took these, each found by comparing at 8x:

- **Few colours**: each tuft stands on the integer base step the base layer shows at its
  root.  Before this, tufts rounded their own base and a stroke one step darker than a 5 could
  be a 4 painted on a 4: invisible.  Whole flat patches of the meadow turned out to be
  covered in invisible strokes.
- **Minority strokes**: stroke sign from where the tuft's value sits in its family.
- **Deep darks grow with stroke size** for strands (a -2 or -3 stroke only when ell > 2-4),
  but **far dots on lit ground are holes** (-3) (section 0, point 3).
- **Step 6 only on crests**; everywhere else lit strokes top out at 5.
- **Twice as directional as nature**: spread and wind lean small; Ferrari's grass is
  measured ~2x more vertical than photos.
- **Stats vs the tarn lit bank** (`src/stats.py`, 60x50 crops): colours for 90% 4 vs 3,
  base share 0.37 vs 0.52, vertical run 2.9 vs 3.5 px, horizontal run 1.7 vs 1.6.

**Silhouettes.**  A tuft with background just above its root (water, sky, ground well
behind) is a *silhouette tuft*: its blades are 1.8x longer (ungrazed lips, full height seen
edge-on), vary 3:1 in height, curve more, and only the 1-px blade line is drawn (partner and
gap columns thickened the fringe into a hedge).  Every blade pixel over a background is
**counterchanged**: dark against light water or sky, light against the dark mirrored bank.
What the water shows behind a blade is itself computed (`painter.water_background`: the
mirrored bank or the sky tint), because the first version pushed blades dark against dark
reflections and the fringe vanished.

---

## 4. Mid and far: the handoff inside one bank (stage 2)

![perspective](img/stage2_perspective_bank.png)

Bands of growing ell (0.5 px at the top to 9 px at the bottom), beside Ferrari's tarn bank
crest to near.  The top now matches: isolated black holes on ochre.  My first several
versions used balanced +-1 dots far off (tone-preserving, and wrong: soup of five colours) and
then shallow brown dots (calm, and wrong: Ferrari keeps only the extremes far off).

**Not there yet**: his near strands are denser, longer and more continuous than mine (the
band crop truncates my strokes a little, but not enough to explain it), and his middle zone
has a brown tick band that reads as a distinct darker belt; mine is more uniform.

---

## 5. The zoom (stage 3)

![strip](img/zoom_golden_strip.png)

Eight frames of the 96 (30, 15.9, 8.4, 4.4, 2.3, 1.2, 0.64, 0.3 px/m).  Tone pops: max 0.6-0.8
luminance levels between frames (mean ~0.2); structure changes stay at the resampling floor.

---

## 6. Parameters

`World(seed, species, moisture, grazing, wind, wind_dir, season_dead, relief, hummock)`,
`Camera(ppm, theta, W, H, cx, cy, cz)`, `paint(world, cam, hour, vparams, mparams)`.

- **species**: turf, meadow, hay, tussock, sedge (height, tuft spacing and width, tussock
  fraction, stiffness).  `sheets/sheet_B_species_x_distance.png`
- **moisture / grazing**: wetness darkens and greens, dryness turns patches to straw (a
  second ramp, applied by patch, never as speckle); grazing shortens the sward, removes
  tussocks, adds straw.  `sheet_C`
- **wind**: lean and combing sheen bands.  `sheet_D`
- **hour**: day, golden, dusk, overcast (palettes; sun direction).  `sheet_A`
- **camera angle**: 12 to 90 degrees.  `sheet_E`
- **seed**: `sheet_F`
- `MarkParams`: coverage curve, blades per px, spread, droop, pair/gap chances, dot depth,
  detail-at-edges strength, clumping, crown light, silhouette length, counterchange step,
  band width, tone lock and its reference stroke length.

print("tone", np.round(t, 2)); print("struct", np.round(s, 2))

Bash: Fixed-CDF stable threshold; retest
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && python3 - <<'EOF'
n=open('grasslod/noise.py').read()
i=n.index("def stable_threshold")
n=n[:i]+'''_CDF = None


def _cdf_table():
    """Empirical CDF of the normalised two-octave blend used by stable_threshold, so the
    mapping to [0,1) is a fixed function (a per-frame rank would depend on what else is
    in the frame, and pixels near the threshold would flicker as the view changes)."""
    global _CDF
    if _CDF is None:
        rs = np.random.RandomState(9)
        x, y = rs.uniform(0, 4000, 200000), rs.uniform(0, 4000, 200000)
        vals = []
        for f in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5):
            a = value_noise(x, y, 3)
            b = value_noise(x / 2, y / 2, 5)
            vals.append((a * (1 - f) + b * f) / np.sqrt((1 - f) ** 2 + f ** 2))
        v = np.sort(np.concatenate(vals))
        _CDF = v[:: max(1, len(v) // 4096)]
    return _CDF


def stable_threshold(X, Y, ppm, px=1.6, seed=5):
    """Dither threshold anchored to the world surface (no shower-door shimmer on zoom).

    Value noise with a lattice ~px screen pixels, living in world coordinates.  As the
    camera zooms, the lattice level (a power of two in world metres) changes; the two
    nearest levels are cross-faded by the fractional zoom octave, so the pattern grows
    and divides continuously instead of swimming or popping (cf. surface-stable
    fractal dithering).  The blend is variance-normalised and mapped through a fixed CDF
    to uniform [0,1).
    """
    cell = px / ppm
    lv = np.log2(cell)
    k = np.floor(lv)
    f = lv - k
    a = value_noise(X / 2 ** k, Y / 2 ** k, seed + int(k) * 3)
    b = value_noise(X / 2 ** (k + 1), Y / 2 ** (k + 1), seed + int(k + 1) * 3)
    v = (a * (1 - f) + b * f) / np.sqrt((1 - f) ** 2 + f ** 2)
    cdf = _cdf_table()
    return (np.searchsorted(cdf, v) + 0.5) / (len(cdf) + 1)
'''
open('grasslod/noise.py','w').write(n)
EOF
nice python3 zoomtest.py 24.7 19.4 6 golden 2>&1 | tail -2

Bash: Churn experiment: fixed keep
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && cat > churn.py <<'EOF'
"""churn.py: measure per-frame churn of the lit meadow at near zoom under experiments."""
import sys, numpy as np
from PIL import Image
from zoom import lum, blur
from grasslod.world import World
from grasslod.render import Camera
from grasslod import painter, marks
from grasslod import palette as P

def run(label, p0=24.7, p1=21.0, n=4, patch=None):
    w = World(seed=1); cy, cz = painter.centre_on_bank(w)
    fr = []; ppms = np.geomspace(p0, p1, n)
    for q in ppms:
        cam = Camera(ppm=q, theta=30, W=320, H=180, cx=0, cy=cy, cz=cz)
        idx, pal = painter.paint(w, cam, "golden")
        fr.append(lum(P.to_rgb(idx, pal)))
    out = []
    for k in range(n - 1):
        A, B = fr[k], fr[k + 1]; r = ppms[k + 1] / ppms[k]; H, W = A.shape
        As = np.array(Image.fromarray(A.astype(np.float32)).resize((int(round(W * r)), int(round(H * r))), Image.BOX))
        h, w_ = As.shape; y0, x0 = (H - h) // 2, (W - w_) // 2; Bc = B[y0:y0 + h, x0:x0 + w_]
        out.append(np.abs(blur(As) - blur(Bc))[h // 2 + 10:-6].mean())
    print(label, np.round(out, 2), flush=True)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    exp = sys.argv[1] if len(sys.argv) > 1 else "base"
    if exp == "fixkeep":
        marks.keep_for = lambda *a, **k: 0.15
        painter.keep_for = marks.keep_for
    run(exp)
EOF
nice python3 churn.py base & nice python3 churn.py fixkeep & wait

        def vd(R, sun, params=None):

against = (bgdep > D + mp["silh_gap"]) | (R.mat[Y, X] != 1)
    if against.any() and getattr(R, "bg_value", None) is not None:
        bv = R.bg_value[Y, X]
        vv = VV
        # against light (sky tint, lit ground) go dark; against dark (mirrored bank,
        # shadowed ground) go light
        push_dark = np.where(R.mat[Y, X] != 1, bv >= 3.0, bv >= vv - 0.5)
        # compress the blade's own range into the darks (keeps two values of structure)
        dark = np.minimum(np.minimum(vv, (vv - 3.0) * 0.5 + 1.6), bv - mp["cc_step"])
        newv = np.where(push_dark, dark, np.minimum(np.maximum(vv, bv + mp["cc_step"]), 5.0))
        VV = np.where(against, np.clip(newv, 0, 6), VV)
        # against the background only the blade's own 1-px line shows: partner and gap
        # columns (layers 0,1) would thicken the fringe into a hedge

        X, Y, VV, K, M = X[keepc], Y[keepc], VV[keepc], K[keepc], M[keepc]

    np.maximum.at(prio, (Y, X), K)
    win = prio[Y, X] == K
    X, Y, VV, M = X[win], Y[win], VV[win], M[win]

Bash: Synthetic floor for stable strokes
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/src && python3 -c "
import numpy as np; from PIL import Image
from zoom import blur
rs=np.random.RandomState(0); N=9000
X=rs.uniform(-8,8,N); Y=rs.uniform(-5,5,N); Lw=rs.uniform(0.2,0.3,N); val=rs.choice([60.,100.,20.],N)
def rend(p,W=320,H=180):
    im=np.full((H,W),150.)
    o=np.argsort(-Y)
    for i in o:
        x=int(np.floor(X[i]*p+W/2)); y0=int(np.floor(-Y[i]*p*0.5+H/2)); L=int(np.floor(Lw[i]*p*0.87+0.5))
        if 0<=x<W: im[max(0,y0-L):max(0,min(H,y0)),x]=val[i]
    return im

cat > tests.py <<'EOF'
"""tests.py: regression checks for the handoff machinery.

1. pan stability: panning the camera by whole pixels must reproduce the same pixels.
   (This caught two array-alignment bugs that made silhouettes and stroke colours
   quasi-random, i.e. boiling on zoom, while every single frame looked plausible.)
2. tone continuity: the flat-patch mean tone across the ell ladder stays within 0.25 steps
   of the reference (tone lock).
"""
import numpy as np
from grasslod.world import World
from grasslod.render import Camera
from grasslod import painter


def pan(ppm=22.0, hour="golden", theta=30):
    w = World(seed=1); cy, cz = painter.centre_on_bank(w)
    fr = []
    for dx in (0, 3):
        cam = Camera(ppm=ppm, theta=theta, W=240, H=140, cx=dx / ppm, cy=cy, cz=cz)
        idx, _ = painter.paint(w, cam, hour)
        fr.append(idx)
    d = (fr[0][:, 3:] != fr[1][:, :-3])[:, 12:-12].mean()
    print(f"pan stability ppm={ppm}: differing pixels {d:.4f}", "OK" if d < 0.002 else "FAIL")
    return d


def tone():
    import vocab
    from grasslod import palette as P
    ells = [14, 6, 2.8, 1.4, 0.7, 0.3, 0.12]
    for v in (4.8, 4.1):
        ms = []
        for e in ells:
            t = vocab.flat_patch(e, v, True, "golden", W=96, H=72)
            pal, _ = P.build("golden")
            lut = {tuple(c): i for i, c in enumerate(pal)}
            q = np.array([lut[tuple(c)] - P.GREEN0 for c in t.reshape(-1, 3)])
            ms.append(q.mean())
        ms = np.array(ms)
        print(f"tone V={v}: mean step by ell {np.round(ms, 2)}  spread {ms.max() - ms.min():.2f}")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    for p in (22.0, 6.0, 1.5):
        pan(p)
    tone()
EOF

# Critique (newest first)

## Round 10 — 2026-09-26 01:36 — `stage0_ferrari_distance_ladder.png`, sheets B–F, LESSONS (section 7 still "to be completed")
Your Ferrari ladder has the answer to the mid distance in its bottom-left crop: **"V16 mid bank, 3-green checker domes."** His middle distance is *ordered* checker dither (a regular 2×2 pattern shaped into domes, three greens), not random stipple. Your 3–5 px/m frames are random stipple, which is why they read as sandpaper while his read as grass slopes. Try an ordered Bayer checker between two adjacent tones, shaped by a dome/drift field, for the middle band. (The ground student found the same thing in their compare sheet.) (My read; your call.)

Sheet E (camera × distance): theta 55 and 90 at 3 and 0.8 px/m now have good big masses; theta 12 at 3 px/m is still sandpaper.


## Round 9 — 2026-09-26 01:19 — `stage2_perspective_bank.png`, sheets A–F
**A correction to my round 8 note first.** Your perspective-bank comparison (Ferrari's tarn bank, crest → near) shows that his farthest grass *is* scattered single dark pixels on flat ochre. So "never go below a 1×2 tick" was wrong as a rule. What his crest has and yours doesn't is **only two tones**: dark dots on a flat base, nothing else. And between the crest and the near strands there's a **mid-olive band of short vertical strokes** (a mass!) that your bank lacks entirely. Yours is three- or four-tone noise from top to bottom. So: two tones at the crest, a distinct mid band of short verticals, long strands near. (My read; your call.)

Sheet C: **the moisture rows are nearly identical** (dry 0.1, mesic 0.5 and wet 0.9 look the same at every grazing level). If moisture is meant to change colour or density, it isn't reaching the pixels. It's also exactly the material field that could give day and overcast their masses.

Sheet A: day and overcast at 3 and 0.8 px/m are still flat speckle.


## Round 8 — 2026-09-26 01:02 — `img/vocab_golden.png`, `zoom_golden`, `wip2`
**The vocabulary sheet puts the whole problem in one image.** (My read; your call.) Read left to right: at ell 14 → 4 px the marks are vertical strands (grass). At ell 2.8 → 2 they turn into isotropic single pixels, and from ell 2 down it's sandpaper. So the handoff from strand to noise happens around ell 2–3 px, which is exactly your middle distance.

The fix I'd try: **never go below a 1×2 vertical tick.** From ell 2 down, keep 1×2 ticks (still vertical) and let their *density* and contrast fall instead, until the field is flat tone with sparse ticks. Ferrari's mid-distance V16 grass does exactly that. Row 2 (lit V=4.1) is also two-tone camo at every ell, the same problem in a different key.

    """Base layer: flat bands of whole steps, dithered only across the seams, with a

def quantize_base(Vd, X, Y, ppm, s, band_w, iy=None, ordered=True):
    """Base layer: flat bands of whole steps, dithered only across the seams.

    Seams use Ferrari's patterned (ordered 4x4) dither, anchored to a world-aligned pixel
    grid: column = floor(X * ppm), row = screen row offset by the camera's own pixel
    origin, so whole-pixel pans reproduce the pattern exactly.  (A checker has no scale,
    so zooming never pops it.)  ordered=False uses the world-noise threshold instead."""
    from .noise import stable_threshold
    lo = np.floor(Vd)
    f = Vd - lo
    g = np.clip((f - band_w / 2) / max(1 - band_w, 1e-3), 0, 1)

        t = BAYER4[iy % 4, ix % 4]
    else:
        t = stable_threshold(X, Y * s, ppm, px=1.0)
    return (lo + (g > t)).astype(int)''')

# Grass at every distance: a study of the handoff

*Grass that zooms continuously from a person 30 px tall (30 px/m) to a whole region
(0.3 px/m) without popping, in Mark Ferrari's painted-pixel language.*

Everything is made by `src/` (package `grasslod/`).  `src/render_all.sh` regenerates every
image (~30 min on 4 threads); `src/tests.py` runs the regression checks.  Paths are relative
to this folder.

**Deliverables**

- `img/zoom_golden.gif`, `img/zoom_day.gif`: 96-frame continuous zooms, 30 to 0.3 px/m,
  shown 2x.  Filmstrips `img/zoom_*_strip.png`, pop metrics `img/zoom_*_metrics.png`,
  single frames `img/zoom_*/fNNN.png`.
- `img/vocab_golden.png`, `img/vocab_day.png`: the mark vocabulary as a function of stroke size.
- `img/stage2_perspective_bank.png`: the handoff inside one receding bank, beside Ferrari's.
- `sheets/sheet_A..F`: hour, species, moisture x grazing, wind, camera angle and seed, each
  across distance.
- The technique as code: `grasslod.paint(world, camera, hour, vparams, mparams)`.

---

## 0. What Ferrari does at each distance

![ladder](img/stage0_ferrari_distance_ladder.png)

I cropped every grass passage I could find in his native pixels (Mirror Pond V16, the tarn,
the rain scene V04) and ordered them by distance.  Read in order, they form one continuous
vocabulary:

| distance | what the pixels are | colours |
|---|---|---|
| near, lit (tarn bank) | 1 px vertical strands, 4-10 px long, of the *darker* steps on a lit base; about half the base shows | 3-4 |
| near, shadow (V16) | 1 px olive strands on black, leaning, curving | 3 |
| crest of a receding bank (tarn) | **isolated black holes** on flat ochre, then a **dense band of brown 1x2-1x4 ticks**, then strands | 2, then 3 |
| lit crest over a shadowed body (V16) | a picket of bright ticks along the crest only, thinning into the dark body | 4 |
| middle distance, shade (V16 mid bank) | an **ordered checker** of close greens shaped into domes; darker fold bands | 3 |
| far, lit (islet) | 2-colour checker, form turned by the density of the lit colour; fringed crest | 2-3 |
| far slopes (V04) | flat value planes, dither only at their seams, faint fine texture | 2-3 per plane |

Three things in this table decided the design:

1. **The tarn bank already *is* a handoff.**  Top to bottom it runs from crest (far) to
   near, and the vocabulary changes three times inside one mass: holes, then a tick band,
   then strands.  Ferrari solved the handoff within one picture; a zoom has to do the same
   thing over time.
2. **The minority-stroke rule.**  In a lit mass the marks are the darks; in a shadowed mass
   they are the lights.  The base is the majority colour, and it is flat.
3. **Far off, only the extremes survive.**  At the crest the marks are the *deepest* dark
   (the holes between tufts), not the mid tone.  Mid-tone ticks appear only once blades are
   resolvable.  I had this backwards for a long time (section 4).

The photos (`photos/*/_sheet.jpg`) agreed on the structure behind this.  Close up, blades
cross and arc.  At 10 m grass is combed streaks with lighter tips and clumps.  At 100 m it
is patches: straw on dry ground, green in hollows.  From the air it is tone and mottling on
landform, with no blades at all.

---

## 1. The idea: one number per tuft, and every decision dithered

Grass is painted as **tufts**: objects in the world, each with its own sixteen stable random
numbers.  One continuous quantity per tuft decides how it is painted, its projected blade
length in pixels:

    ell = h * ppm * sqrt(cos(theta)^2 + (lean * sin(theta)^3)^2)

Here h is the local blade height and theta the camera elevation.  The second term lets a
top-down camera still see tufts, as small radiating marks.

The vocabulary as a function of ell (`img/vocab_golden.png`):

![vocab](img/vocab_golden.png)

- **ell >= ~4: strands.**  A tuft is a fan of 1 px blades (about ell/1.6 of them), stacked in
  adjacent steps, with a partner column (the stem) and sometimes a dark gap column.  Deep
  darks (-2, -3 steps) appear only once strokes are long enough.
- **ell ~0.6-3: ticks.**  Each mark is at least a 1x2 vertical tick, a single tone one step
  from the base, at high coverage.  This is the dense tick band of the tarn bank, and it
  holds the vertical down to the far bank.
- **ell < ~0.6: holes.**  Single pixels.  On lit ground a dot is a hole (about -2 steps), on
  shadowed ground a light speck, sparse: two tones.
- **Beyond that** the dots thin to ~8 % coverage, and the base layer (flat planes on lit
  ground, ordered checker on shaded ground) carries the picture.

**The handoff rule.**  Every *discrete* choice (how many blades, how many whole pixels long,
pair or no pair, gap or no gap, dark stroke or light, tick or dot, drawn or dropped) compares
a smooth function of ell against *one of that tuft's own random numbers*.  So as the camera
zooms, each tuft changes at its own moment, and the vocabulary dissolves instead of
switching.  It is dithering, applied to the mark vocabulary itself.  The one hard threshold
left in the first version (a width term in the coverage estimate, at ell = 3) showed up in
the metric as a single tone spike at 12.5 px/m, and removing it removed the spike.

**Population.**  Tufts live on a nested hierarchy of jittered grids.  Level k has one tuft
per cell of size s0 * 2^k, with ranks in [4^-(k+1), 4^-k).  Keeping ranks below K keeps a
fraction K of all tufts, the kept set only grows as K grows, and at a distance only the
coarse levels need to be enumerated.  Zooming out, tufts drop out one by one, lowest rank
last, and each survivor stands for more grass.

**Coverage is designed, then measured.**  The fraction of pixels covered by marks follows a
smooth design curve: 0.08 far, **0.62 in the tick band** (ell 1.6-3), 0.55 for strands.  How
many tufts that takes can't be computed, because blades of a tuft overlap and marks overlap
marks.  So it is measured once on a flat patch, fitted as coverage = 1 - exp(-a * keep), and
the rate a(ell) is stored and inverted (`calibrate.coverage_ratio`).  My first estimate used
a linear ratio, and near strands came out at 40 % instead of 58 %: overlap saturates.

**Tone: a partial lock toward the far value.**  Each vocabulary has a different mean offset
from the design value: dark gaps pull a lit mass down, and clipped highlights can't push it
up.  I measure the offset once on flat patches, and paint with
V' = V - lock * (offset(ell) - offset(far)).  At first I locked fully to the *near* tone, so
the far base drifted into a two-step checker and the crest lost its flat ochre.  Now the
design value is the far look (flat base plus sparse holes), and half of the near strands'
darkening is compensated.  The squinted tone drifts smoothly by about half a step between
far and near, in the same direction as Ferrari's bank (darker near, where the gaps
resolve), and never jumps.

**World-anchored everything.**  Terrain and every ecological field are band-limited
functions of world position: octaves below two pixels fade out, never cut.  Raster sample
rows sit on a global grid.  The base layer's dither is an ordered 4x4 pattern on a
world-aligned pixel grid.  The remaining noise thresholds (terminators, straw, soil, water
dashes) use `noise.stable_threshold`: world value noise whose lattice level cross-fades
between powers of two as the zoom changes (the surface-stable fractal dithering idea),
mapped through a fixed CDF.  My first version rank-normalised per frame, which let the
rest of the frame move each pixel's threshold.  Nothing is screen-random, so nothing
shimmers.

---

## 2. Testing the handoff

**Pops.**  `zoom.py` shrinks each frame about its centre and compares it with the next
frame's centre: tone = |mean luminance difference|, structure = mean |blur2 difference|.

![metrics](img/zoom_golden_metrics.png)

**Pan stability** (`tests.py`).  This became my most useful test.  Panning the camera by
whole pixels must reproduce the same pixels.  It failed at 7 %, and tracing the differing
pixels found two array-alignment bugs in `paint_tufts`.  After the depth sort, the
silhouette probe read the wrong tuft's screen column.  After the detail drop, the clump
"crown" was indexed against the unfiltered list.  So silhouettes and stroke colours were
quasi-random functions of which other tufts happened to be on screen.  Each frame looked
plausible, and the zoom boiled.  The near churn metric fell from 7.4 to 5.3, and pan
differences are now exactly 0 at 22, 6 and 1.5 px/m.

**How much churn is left, and is it mine?**  Same-content, same-contrast synthetic strokes
(fixed world segments, only re-rasterised) score 3.8 on the churn metric at the same zoom
step, so most of what's left at 20-30 px/m is inherent to 1 px strokes being re-rasterised.
Experiments that froze each suspect (keep, tone lock, curvature) changed it by less than
0.2.  The critic (rounds 2, 7, 11) looked frame by frame at the places where my metrics
spike and found no pops.

**Latest run:** golden: tone pop max 0.6, mean 0.2 luminance levels per frame; day: max 0.8.

---

## 3. The value design is the painting (and the far views are nothing else)

`render.value_design` gives a continuous step coordinate V for every pixel.  Here is what I
had to add before anything read, in the order I found it:

- **Two families that never touch.**  Lit covers steps 3-5; step 6 is reserved for
  sun-struck tips on crests.  Shadow covers 0.2-2.8.  The family boundary is dithered by a
  world-anchored threshold over a soft light weight, so terminators and shadow edges are
  fringes, not cut-outs.
- **Exaggerated facing**: slope-to-sun differences relative to flat ground, x2 at pixel
  scale and again at ~30 px (bank and hill scale).  Physical Lambert shading on an ortho
  30 degree view is flat everywhere, and a gentle hummock must read.
- **Masses made of masses**: convexity summed at 6, 16 and 48 px.  Crests are lighter and
  folds darker, at every zoom.
- **Material masses that belong to the ground, not the light** (critic round 6): a 10-80 m
  field of drier knolls (lighter) and wet flushes (darker), plus 8-60 m mottling.  Without
  these, day and overcast had no structure at 3 and 0.8 px/m, because all the masses came
  from low-sun shadows.
- **Moisture reaches the pixels** (critic round 9): dry ground turns to the straw ramp in
  patches (never as speckle), and wet ground darkens and greens (`sheet_C`).  My first
  wetness field was dominated by "near the stream", and every test frame is centred on
  the stream.
- **Cast shadows fixed in world units** (a geometric march from 5 cm to 80 m), and
  **simplified like a painter's**: relief smaller than 8 px casts no shadow.  Two bugs here
  were instructive.  When the march reach scaled with zoom, things fell into shadow as you
  zoomed out: a slow pop.  When the ray started at the fine surface but was tested against
  band-limited samples, every fine hollow filled with false shadow, and the far views
  turned to leopard camouflage.
- **Flat planes on lit ground, checker on shaded ground.**  The base is quantized into whole
  steps.  Lit masses dither only in a narrow seam (band 0.7), as his lit ochre is flat.
  Shaded and mid masses use ordered checker everywhere (band 0), as in his V16 mid bank.
  Random stipple there read as sandpaper (critic round 10).

---

## 4. Near: strands, fringes, counterchange (stage 1)

At 30 px/m a meadow blade is 8 px tall (0.3 m seen at 30 degrees).  That puts it in the
tarn-bank regime, not V04's giant curving blades.  Comparing at 8x against the tarn's lit
bank taught me these:

- **Few colours**: each tuft stands on the integer base step the base layer shows at its
  root.  Before this, tufts rounded their own base, so a stroke one step darker than a 5
  could be a 4 painted on a 4: invisible.  Whole "flat" patches of the meadow turned out to
  be covered in invisible strokes.
- **Minority strokes**: the stroke's sign comes from where the tuft's value sits in its
  family.
- **Step 6 only on crests**; everywhere else, lit strokes top out at 5.
- **Twice as directional as nature**: spread and wind lean are small.
  (observation/ measured Ferrari's grass at about twice as directional as photos.)
- **Clumps modulate light and density a great deal far off, and barely up close.**  Close
  up, gaps between clumps are still grass; modulating them hard made bare flat patches.
- **Stats vs the tarn lit bank** (`src/stats.py`, 60x50 crops): colours covering 90 %: 4 vs
  3; base share 0.36 vs 0.52; vertical run 2.9 vs 3.5 px; horizontal run 1.7 vs 1.6.

**Silhouettes.**  A tuft with background just above its root (water, sky, or ground well
behind it) is a *silhouette tuft*.  Its blades are 1.8x longer (ungrazed lips, full height
seen edge-on), vary 3:1 in height and curve more.  Only the 1 px blade line is drawn,
because partner and gap columns thickened the fringe into a hedge.  Every blade pixel over a
background is **counterchanged**: dark against light water or sky, light against the dark
mirrored bank.  What the water shows behind a blade is itself computed
(`painter.water_background`: the mirrored bank, or the sky tint).  The first version pushed
blades dark against dark reflections, and the fringe vanished.

---

## 5. Mid and far: the handoff inside one bank (stage 2)

![perspective](img/stage2_perspective_bank.png)

These are bands of growing ell (0.5 px at the top to 9 px at the bottom), beside Ferrari's
tarn bank from crest to near.  It took five versions.

1. Balanced +-1 dots far off: tone-preserving, and a soup of five colours.
2. Shallow brown dots: calm, and wrong.
3. Deep holes: the crest matched, but the middle was uniform stipple.
4. With the full tone lock and an ordered checker everywhere, the whole column turned into
   one uniform three-tone texture (critic round 11: "changes only its grain size").
5. Now: two tones at the crest (flat ochre and holes), a denser band of short brown ticks,
   and strands below.

**Not there yet.**  His near strands are longer, darker and more continuous (olive runs of
8-15 px).  Mine are shorter and let more base through.  The band crop truncates my strokes a
little, but not enough to explain the difference.  His tick band is also a more distinct
darker belt than mine.

---

## 6. The zoom (stage 3)

![strip](img/zoom_golden_strip.png)

These are eight of the 96 frames (30, 15.9, 8.4, 4.4, 2.3, 1.2, 0.64 and 0.3 px/m).
Watching `img/zoom_golden.gif`:

- the fringe of lit blades against the dark reflection shortens into a ticked lip, then
  into the bank's contact line;
- the lit meadow goes from strands to a tick texture to flat ochre with holes;
- the shaded far slope goes to checker domes;
- the big ground masses come in as the frame widens.

---

## 7. Parameters

`World(seed, species, moisture, grazing, wind, wind_dir, season_dead, relief, hummock)`,
`Camera(ppm, theta, W, H, cx, cy, cz)`, `paint(world, cam, hour, vparams, mparams)`.

- **species**: turf, meadow, hay, tussock or sedge; sets height, tuft spacing and width,
  tussock fraction and stiffness.  `sheets/sheet_B_species_x_distance.png`
- **moisture and grazing**: dryness gives straw patches and lighter knolls; wetness darkens
  and greens.  Grazing shortens the sward, removes tussocks and adds straw.  `sheet_C`
- **wind**: lean and combing sheen bands.  `sheet_D`
- **hour**: day, golden, dusk or overcast (palette and sun direction).  `sheet_A`
- **camera angle**: 12 to 90 degrees; top-down works through the lean term.  `sheet_E`
- **seed**: `sheet_F`
- `MarkParams` (`grasslod/marks.py`): coverage curve (far, tick band, near), blades per px,
  spread, droop, pair and gap chances, dot depth, detail-at-edges strength, clumping, crown
  light, silhouette length, counterchange step, band widths (lit and shadow), tone lock
  strength and reference.
- `vparams` (`render.value_design`): exaggeration (small and large scale), family ranges,
  crest, fold, wetness darkening, mottling, material masses, wind sheen, terminator
  softness, shadow simplification.

---

## 8. Honest state

**What I think works**

- **The handoff.**  One stroke-size number per tuft, with every discrete decision dithered
  against the tuft's own random numbers, gives a zoom with no pops across 100x.  The critic
  checked this frame by frame three times.  The tools that got it there are the pan test,
  the pop metric with its measured floor, and the tone and coverage calibrations; I would
  keep all of them for every other material.
- **A few passages sit in Ferrari's world**:
  - 30 px/m golden: lit strands, a fringe against the reflection, the pink dashes;
  - the far golden and dusk views: big masses, checker seams;
  - the crest of the perspective bank.

**What isn't there yet**

- **Near strands vs his**: shorter, and more base showing (section 5).
- **The shaded far bank at 20-30 px/m** is a dark mush of three near-blacks.  His shadow
  bank has crisper, longer olive strands.
- **Day and overcast** now have masses, but they are still less beautiful than the low-sun
  hours.  Ferrari's midday grass (V16) gets its drama from composition: dark repoussoir
  banks and lit middle ground.  An ortho game camera over a gentle meadow doesn't have that,
  and I don't think the painter can invent it.  This is a question for the director or
  simulator.
- **Low camera (theta 12)**: long diagonal strands everywhere at 30 px/m reads as combed.
- **Water** is a context placeholder: a mirror column, dark tints, world-stable dashes.
- **Speed**: 3-10 s per 320x180 frame in numpy.  A Rust port would need to vectorise the
  per-tuft blade loop.

**What would help**

- **A perspective (not ortho) test camera**, to judge the handoff the way Ferrari's banks
  show it: in one image, from crest to near.  My band emulation truncates strokes.
- **A second Ferrari reference with near grass in daylight**, to calibrate the day palette.
  All my day values are extrapolated from V16's shadowed banks and one lit crest.

Read: elements/grass-lod/img/zoom_day_strip.png

117. elements/grass-lod/img/zoom_day_strip.png

Day zoom now has ground masses all the way through. The deliverables are complete; handing back.

SubagentHandback:

I built grass that zooms from 30 to 0.3 px/m with no pops, and delivered two 96-frame zoom GIFs (golden hour and day). It isn't at Ferrari's level everywhere yet; LESSONS.md, section 8, lists where it falls short.

How the handoff works. Each tuft of grass is placed in the world and has its own fixed random numbers. One number, how many pixels long its blades are on screen, decides how it's drawn:

  • up close: strands;
  • further off: short 1x2 vertical ticks;
  • further still: single dark holes;
  • far off: a light peppering over flat tone.

Every either/or choice (how many blades, how long, tick or dot, drawn or dropped) is compared against that tuft's own random number. So as the camera zooms, each tuft changes at its own moment and one style fades into the next instead of switching all at once. When zooming out, tufts drop out one at a time and the survivors stand for more grass.

How I checked it.

  • Frame-to-frame change: at most 0.53 luminance levels in golden light and 0.69 in day. The critic went through the frames one by one three times and found no pops.
  • Pan test: panning the camera by whole pixels must give identical output. It first failed on 7% of pixels, which exposed two bugs that made the grass edges and stroke colours change randomly between frames, so the zoom "boiled" even though each frame looked fine. After the fixes it passes with zero differences at 22, 6 and 1.5 px/m.
  • What's left: the remaining change between frames is about what perfectly stable 1-pixel strokes produce just from being redrawn at a new scale.

What works.

  • Near grass in golden light: lit strands, and blades along the bank edge that go dark or light depending on what's behind them.
  • A side-by-side with Ferrari's receding tarn bank now shows his three zones: two tones at the far edge, a band of short ticks, then strands.
  • The middle distance uses the regular checker dither Ferrari uses in Mirror Pond.
  • Day and overcast now have big light and dark ground areas, driven by a moisture field, and moisture changes the colours.

Not there yet.

  • My near strands are shorter than Ferrari's and let more of the base colour through.
  • The shadowed far bank up close is a murky mass of near-blacks, where his has crisp olive strands.
  • Midday is less beautiful than low sun. Ferrari gets that drama from composition, which I think is a job for the scene layout rather than the grass painter.
  • A low camera (12°) looks combed.
  • The water is a placeholder.
  • Rendering takes 3–10 seconds per 320x180 frame.

The code. The entry point is grasslod.paint(world, camera, hour, vparams, mparams). The world takes species, moisture, grazing, wind, relief, hummock size and seed. The camera takes pixels per metre and angle (12–90°, top-down works). The hours are day, golden, dusk and overcast. src/render_all.sh regenerates everything in about 30 minutes on 4 threads.

What would help:

  • A perspective test camera: my stand-in for a receding bank cuts strokes short.
  • A Ferrari painting with near grass in daylight, to tune the day colours.

Everything is in ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/grass-lod/. Earlier intermediate images are in img/old/.
